Note the paper total is ~1.3% higher; likely a manuscript-level count from an earlier/pre-release processing state. |\n231\t| Lick artifact threshold | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` invalidates a trial if `>35%` of samples have cumulative lick count `>2` | NWB stores cumulative lick counts per frame | Methods text states `>30%` | Prefer the released code threshold (`35%`) when reproducing continuous-sample processing, and document the text/code mismatch. |\n232\t| Cell count range | Code uses curated `iscell`; later analyses may also exclude putative interneurons | `iscell[:,0]==1` gives 155-2341 accepted cells/session; only three `m18` sessions exceed 2172 | Methods report 155-2172 putative pyramidal neurons/session | Accept `iscell[:,0]==1` as the primary shared-data curation. The slight excess occurs only in pooled multi-plane `m18` sessions and likely reflects release-version differences or later exclusions not baked into NWB. |\n233\t| Putative interneuron exclusion | `dayData` documents exclusion by speed correlation threshold `0.5`; helper `spatial.is_putative_interneuron` defaults to `0.3` unless overridden | NWB does not contain a ready-made interneuron mask | Methods report exclusion for Pearson `r > 0.5` | If interneuron exclusion is needed, use the paper/dayData threshold `0.5`, not the helper’s default `0.3`. |\n234\t| Sampling rate in multi-plane sessions | Code/paper interpret two-plane imaging as ~15.5 Hz per plane | 28 NWB files have `planeIdx` in `{0,1}` and `Deconvolved.rate = 31.015625`, but behavior timestamps have ~0.0645 s spacing and same sample count as neural traces | Paper states two-plane scans were interleaved at 31 Hz for ~15.5 Hz per plane | Use behavior timestamps / shared sample length as the aligned time base (~15.5 Hz effective). Do not trust the NWB ophys `rate` attribute blindly in multi-plane files. |\n235\t| Reward-zone labels | Code infers A/B/C from scene names via string matching | NWB identifiers include scene strings such as `Env1_LocationA_to_C` and cross-environment names like `Env1_A_to_Env2_B` | Paper describes A/B/C zones and environment switches | Code-style scene parsing still works because switch names contain `A_to/B_to/C_to` patterns and the final character identifies the destination zone. |\n236\t\n237\tFinal understanding:\n238\t- The released NWB files are processed, frame-aligned session-level data equivalent in content to what the repo stores inside `sess`.\n239\t- Conversion should therefore avoid re-deriving alignment from raw files and instead preserve the shared frame-aligned time base already present in NWB.\n240\t- The core reference semantics to preserve are:\n241\t  - neuron inclusion from curated `iscell`\n242\t  - trial segmentation from `trial_start` / `teleport`\n243\t  - environment identity from `environment`\n244\t  - reward outcome from reward deliveries within each trial\n245\t  - reward-zone identity from scene/reward-zone metadata consistent with `behavior.get_reward_zones`\n246\t  - running-period masking and lick correction logic from `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` where relevant\n247\t\n248\t---\n249\t\n250\t## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n251\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n252\t\n253\t### Variable Mapping\n254\t| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n255\t|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n256\t| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` + `iscell[:,0]` | `neural` | Filter ROIs where `iscell[:,0] == 1`; transpose from `(T, ROI)` to `(ROI, T)`; segment by complete trials | `multi_anim_sess(..., calc_spks=True)`, `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Use deconvolved activity directly as the NWB equivalent of reference `sess.timeseries['events']`. |\n257\t| behavior timestamps within each trial | `input[0]` (`time_from_trial_start_sec`) | `timestamps[start:stop] - timestamps[start]` | frame-aligned continuous sampling in `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Time-varying continuous input. |\n258\t| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment/data` | `input[1]` (`environment`) | Take per-trial modal value in `{0,1}` and repeat across timepoints | `behavior.get_trial_types` (`morph`) | Binary per-trial input, repeated across time. |\n259\t| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/trial number/data` or trial index | `input[2]` (`trial_number`) | Use per-trial integer index (0-based within session), repeated across timepoints | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` (`trial_ids`) | Prefer complete-trial order used in segmentation. |\n260\t| prior trial reward outcome | `input[3]` (`previous_trial_rewarded`) | Compute from previous complete trial, `0` for first kept trial in a session; repeat across timepoints | derived from `behavior.get_trial_types` logic | Binary per-trial input, repeated across time. |\n261\t| `position` + trial-specific reward-zone start/end | `output[0]` (`distance_to_reward_zone`) | Signed nearest distance to zone: `<start => pos-start`, `inside => 0`, `>end => pos-end`, then discretize to 7 bins | reward-zone semantics from `behavior.get_reward_zones`; relative-position logic from `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Bins: `<-50`, `[-50,-10]`, `[-10,0)`, `0`, `(0,10]`, `(10,50]`, `>50` cm. |\n262\t| `position` | `output[1]` (`absolute_position_bin`) | Discretize absolute track position on `[0,450]` into 5 equal bins | task description in paper; `glmUtils` position handling | Time-varying categorical output. |\n263\t| `speed` | `output[2]` (`speed_bin`) | Discretize cm/s into bins `<2`, `2-10`, `10-20`, `20-40`, `>40` | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` uses the same speed signal | Keep low-speed samples because the target task explicitly includes the `<2` class. |\n264\t| `lick` | `output[3]` (`lick`) | Convert cumulative lick count per frame to binary (`>0 => 1`), after trial-level lick-error filtering | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` | Time-varying binary output. |\n265\t| session scene / switch structure | `output[4]` (`reward_zone_location`) | Map trial to zone label `A/B/C`, encode as `0/1/2`, repeat across timepoints | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | For switch sessions, use pre-switch zone for trials `<30`, post-switch zone for trials `>=30`. |\n266\t| reward delivery within trial | `output[5]` (`reward_outcome`) | Trial is rewarded if any reward delivery occurs within the trial and the trial has an `rzone` entry event; encode `0/1`, repeat across timepoints | `behavior.get_trial_types` | Per-trial categorical output, repeated across time. |\n267\t\n268\t### Key Decisions\n269\t1. **Use NWB deconvolved traces directly**: The paper’s RR decoder uses deconvolved calcium events, and the NWB `Deconvolved` data are the released equivalent of reference `sess.timeseries['events']`.\n270\t2. **Filter neurons with `iscell[:,0] == 1` only**: This is the shared, explicit curated-cell mask available in NWB and matches the baseline neuron curation used before downstream analyses.\n271\t3. **Do not recompute dF/F from fluorescence**: The release already provides frame-aligned deconvolved traces; recomputation would add unnecessary divergence from the released data product.\n272\t4. **Segment trials from `trial_start` to `teleport` and exclude teleport periods**: This matches the reference code’s trial-epoch logic and the target requirement to align to trial start.\n273\t5. **Keep only complete trials with explicit start and end markers**: This avoids fabricating incomplete boundaries; for example the clipped first `m11` day-3 trial will be excluded.\n274\t6. **Represent all inputs and outputs as 2D `(d, T)` arrays**: Although some labels are per-trial, repeating them across timepoints makes dimensions uniform and matches `train_decoder.py` well.\n275\t7. **Use actual timestamps for time-from-start and nominal frame size for metadata**: Behavior timestamps are the reliable aligned time base, especially in multi-plane sessions where the ophys `rate` attribute can be misleading.\n276\t8. **Use paper/code reward-zone semantics rather than inferring from sparse `reward_zone` events alone**: The `reward_zone` series marks zone entry events, not the full zone extent, so zone A/B/C must come from session condition metadata.\n277\t9. **Keep low-speed in-trial samples**: The paper’s Fig. 3 RR decoder filtered to `>2 cm/s`, but the requested target outputs explicitly include a `<2 cm/s` class, so full trial-resolved behavior must be retained.\n278\t10. **Drop lick-artifact trials entirely rather than leaving NaNs**: The validator forbids NaNs, and the reference code already treats these trials as invalid for licking analyses.\n279\t11. **Set first-trial previous outcome to 0**: There is no previous within-session trial, so `0` is the least assumption-laden sentinel compatible with the requested binary variable.\n280\t12. **Sort sessions by file path (`subject`, then `session`)**: This gives stable ordering for `subjects`, `subject_idx`, and reproducible outputs.\n281\t\n282\t### session ordering, and `subject_idx` match file paths exactly.\n289\t\n290\t---\n291\t\n292\t## Step 6: Script Development\n293\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n294\t\n295\tImplemented `convert_data.py` with the timepoints makes dimensions uniform and matches `train_decoder.py` well.\n7. **Use actual timestamps for time-from-start and nominal frame size for metadata**: Behavior timestamps are the reliable aligned time base, especially in multi-plane sessions where the ophys `rate` attribute can be misleading.\n8. **Use paper/code reward-zone semantics rather than inferring from sparse `reward_zone` events alone**: The `reward_zone` series marks zone entry events, not the full zone extent, so zone A/B/C must come from session condition metadata.\n9. **Keep low-speed in-trial samples**: The paper’s Fig. 3 RR decoder filtered to `>2 cm/s`, but the requested target outputs explicitly include a `<2 cm/s` class, so full trial-resolved behavior must be retained.\n10. **Drop lick-artifact trials entirely rather than leaving NaNs**: The validator forbids NaNs, and the reference code already treats these trials as invalid for licking analyses.\n11. **Set first-trial previous outcome to 0**: There is no previous within-session trial, so `0` is the least assumption-laden sentinel compatible with the requested binary variable.\n12. **Sort sessions by file path (`subject`, then `session`)**: This gives stable ordering for `subjects`, `subject_idx`, and reproducible outputs.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural spot check: for selected session/trial/neuron/timepoint, confirm converted `neural` value equals the raw NWB `Deconvolved` sample after `iscell` filtering and trial slicing using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Input time-base check: confirm converted `time_from_trial_start_sec` equals raw behavior timestamps minus trial-start timestamp for several trials using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Output position check: confirm converted absolute-position bins and reward-zone-distance bins match raw `position` and manually computed discretization for selected trials using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Output reward check: confirm converted trial reward outcome matches raw reward events within trial bounds for selected trials using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Session-count check: confirm converted session/trial totals match kept complete trials from NWB after lick-artifact exclusion.\n- [ ] Subject/session consistency check: confirm subject list, session ordering, and `subject_idx` match file paths exactly.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nImplemented `convert_data.py` with the required CLI:\n- `python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`\n- `--full` / `--sample`\n- `--show-processing`\n\nImplementation notes:\n- Reads NWB directly with `h5py` for speed.\n- Filters curated ROIs using `iscell[:,0] == 1`.\n- Uses released deconvolved traces as the neural signal.\n- Segments complete trials from `trial_start` to `teleport`.\n- Drops lick-artifact trials using the reference-code-style `>35%` rule.\n- Builds time-varying `(d, T)` input and output arrays for every kept trial.\n- Repeats per-trial labels across timepoints for validator compatibility.\n- Saves diagnostic plots for up to 2 sessions in `--show-processing` mode.\n- Stores session-level summary metadata for later consistency checks.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Full-session deconvolved matrices are still loaded into memory one session at a time.\n- Reward-zone labels are parsed from scene metadata rather than cached lookup tables.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Uses `h5py` instead of higher-level NWB objects for the main conversion path.\n- Processes sessions sequentially and writes only the final pickle, avoiding intermediate I/O.\n- Filters trials before building output arrays to avoid wasted allocations for dropped trials.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and The subject ID is extracted from the directory name.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_subjects(datadir=DATADIR):\n    subjects = [d.split('-')[1] for d in os.listdir(datadir) if d.startswith('sub-')]\n    return subjects\n```\n\niii. The number of subject directories (11) matches the paper. Each directory name follows `sub-<id>` convention.\n\n## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. Each NWB file within a subject's directory corresponds to one session. Session numbers are parsed from filenames matching `sub-<subject>_ses-<NN>_behavior+ophys.nwb`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_sessions(subject, datadir=DATADIR):\n    subject_dir = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\")\n    nwb_files = [f for f in os.listdir(subject_dir) if f.endswith('.nwb')]\n    sessions = [int(f.split('_')[1].split('-')[1]) for f in nwb_files]\n    return sessions\n```\n\niii. File naming convention includes a parsable `ses-<number>` indicating session identity. Total of 152 sessions matches expected count (10 mice x 14 + m11 x 12).\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trial boundaries are defined using the `trial_start` and `teleport` behavior time series. Trial starts are identified where `trial_start` is nonzero. Trial ends are identified at rising edges of the `teleport` signal (where teleport transitions from <=0 to >0).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_trial_starts_ends(trial_start, teleport):\n    trial_start_idx = np.nonzero(trial_start)[0]\n    is_teleport_start = (teleport[1:] > 0) & (teleport[:-1] <= 0)\n    trial_end_idx = np.nonzero(is_teleport_start)[0] + 1\n    assert len(trial_start_idx) == len(trial_end_idx)\n    return trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx\n```\n\niii. The AI investigated using the `trial_number` variable but found it inconsistent with `trial_start`. The `trial_start`/`teleport` approach matches the reference code's trial epoch definition and the paper's description of trials.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI attempts to filter trials with fewer than 50 timepoints using a minimum timepoint threshold. No lick-artifact filtering is implemented.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    if idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n        print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n        istrial[trial] = False\n        continue\n```\n\niii. The AI noted that some short trials existed. However, the code has a bug: `idx.sum()` computes the sum of index values (e.g., [100, 101, 102, ...] sums to thousands), not the number of timepoints (`len(idx)`). This means the filter effectively never activates. Additionally, the reference code and paper describe lick-artifact trial filtering (>35% of frames with lick count > 2), which is not implemented.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. The paper describes training decoders on deconvolved calcium activity data, which corresponds to the NWB `Deconvolved` data.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. The only processing is combining cells from multiple imaging planes for multi-plane sessions and filtering by `iscell`. No frame-level validity filtering is applied. No dF/F recomputation or additional processing is done.\n\nii.\n```python\ndeconvolved_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Deconvolved\"]\nspike_data = []\nnplanes = len(deconvolved_data.roi_response_series)\nfor plane_data in deconvolved_data.roi_response_series.values():\n    roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n    iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n    spike_data_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n    spike_data.append(spike_data_curr[:,iscell])\nspike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n```\n\niii. The AI reasoned that deconvolved data are already processed and no further transformation is needed. The reference code additionally filters out individual frames with NaN or invalid values, which this code does not do.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Neural data is filtered using the `iscell` variable. Only ROIs where `iscell[:,0] == 1` (accepted cells from suite2p curation) are kept.\n\nii.\n```python\nroi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\niscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\nspike_data_curr = plane_data.data[:]\nspike_data.append(spike_data_curr[:,iscell])\n```\n\niii. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. No rebinning is applied. The data is kept at the original sampling rate of ~15.5 Hz (~64.5 ms per frame). Reward zone assignment uses Viterbi with Gaussian emission on distance to known zone ranges:\n```python\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130], 'B': [200, 250], 'C': [320, 370]}\nsigma = 10.0\nzone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])\ndist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                  startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1])\ndist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\nlog_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n\n# Transition encourages stability:\npstay = .9\nlog_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\nnp.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n```\n\niii. The AI plotted reward zone positions and found that the start position of reward zone activation was the cleanest signal for inferring zone labels. The Viterbi algorithm encourages stable zone assignments across consecutive trials, producing approximately 1/3 representation for each zone.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before it, positive when past it.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0]\n    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1]\n    return distance_to_reward_zone\n```\n\niii. Matches the paper's concept of distance relative to the reward zone. The same zone coordinate dictionary as the reference code is used.\n\n## 7-c. The number of subject directories (11) matches the paper. Each directory name follows `sub-<id>` convention.\n\n## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. Each NWB file within a subject's directory corresponds to one session. Session numbers are parsed from filenames matching `sub-<subject>_ses-<NN>_behavior+ophys.nwb`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_sessions(subject, datadir=DATADIR):\n    subject_dir = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\")\n    nwb_files = [f for f in os.listdir(subject_dir) if f.endswith('.nwb')]\n    sessions = [int(f.split('_')[1].split('-')[1]) for f in nwb_files]\n    return sessions\n```\n\niii. File naming convention includes a parsable `ses-<number>` indicating session identity. Total of 152 sessions matches expected count (10 mice x 14 + m11 x 12).\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trial boundaries are defined using the `trial_start` and `teleport` behavior time series. Trial starts are identified where `trial_start` is nonzero. Trial ends are identified at rising edges of the `teleport` signal (where teleport transitions from <=0 to >0).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_trial_starts_ends(trial_start, teleport):\n    trial_start_idx = np.nonzero(trial_start)[0]\n    is_teleport_start = (teleport[1:] > 0) & (teleport[:-1] <= 0)\n    trial_end_idx = np.nonzero(is_teleport_start)[0] + 1\n    assert len(trial_start_idx) == len(trial_end_idx)\n    return trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx\n```\n\niii. The AI investigated using the `trial_number` variable but found it inconsistent with `trial_start`. The `trial_start`/`teleport` approach matches the reference code's trial epoch definition and the paper's description of trials.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI attempts to filter trials with fewer than 50 timepoints using a minimum timepoint threshold. No lick-artifact filtering is implemented.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    if idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n        print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n        istrial[trial] = False\n        continue\n```\n\niii. The AI noted that some short trials existed. However, the code has a bug: `idx.sum()` computes the sum of index values (e.g., [100, 101, 102, ...] sums to thousands), not the number of timepoints (`len(idx)`). This means the filter effectively never activates. Additionally, the reference code and paper describe lick-artifact trial filtering (>35% of frames with lick count > 2), which is not implemented.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. The paper describes training decoders on deconvolved calcium activity data, which corresponds to the NWB `Deconvolved` data.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. The only processing is combining cells from multiple imaging planes for multi-plane sessions and filtering by `iscell`. No frame-level validity filtering is applied. No dF/F recomputation or additional processing is done.\n\nii.\n```python\ndeconvolved_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Deconvolved\"]\nspike_data = []\nnplanes = len(deconvolved_data.roi_response_series)\nfor plane_data in deconvolved_data.roi_response_series.values():\n    roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n    iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n    spike_data_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n    spike_data.append(spike_data_curr[:,iscell])\nspike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n```\n\niii. The AI reasoned that deconvolved data are already processed and no further transformation is needed. The reference code additionally filters out individual frames with NaN or invalid values, which this code does not do.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Neural data is filtered using the `iscell` variable. Neural and behavioral data share the same frame-aligned time base in the NWB files, so trial start alignment is achieved by indexing.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. No rebinning is applied. The data is kept at the original sampling rate of ~15.5 Hz (~64.5 ms per frame). For multi-plane sessions, the effective rate is computed as `nplanes / plane_data.rate * 1000` ms.\n\nii.\n```python\nneural_time_bin_size = nplanes/plane_data.rate*1000 # ms\nassert np.isclose(neural_time_bin_size, time_bin_size, rtol=1e-2)\n```\n\niii. Reward zone assignment uses Viterbi with Gaussian emission on distance to known zone ranges:\n```python\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130], 'B': [200, 250], 'C': [320, 370]}\nsigma = 10.0\nzone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])\ndist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                  startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1])\ndist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\nlog_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n\n# Transition encourages stability:\npstay = .9\nlog_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\nnp.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n```\n\niii. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before it, positive when past it.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0]\n    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1]\n    return distance_to_reward_zone\n```\n\niii. Matches the paper's concept of distance relative to the reward zone. The same zone coordinate dictionary as the reference code is used.\n\n## 7-c.
